# B13 source quotations

1000 current actual questions. Source ledgers record actual authorship, licensing/credit, retrieval timestamps/method/HTTP status and content hashes. Every selected fact is associated with two source accounts; final current acceptance coverage remains in the version-bound report. Full copyrighted captures are excluded.

## B13-0001 — Which city is the state capital of Alabama?

Answer: Montgomery. Fun fact: Alabama uses AL as its two-letter postal abbreviation.

- us-geography-s0001: [Wikipedia contributors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_states_and_territories_of_the_United_States)
  quote: “Alabama AL Montgomery” (3 words).
- us-geography-s0002: [State Symbols USA](https://statesymbolsusa.org/symbol-official-item/national-us/state-capital/state-capitals-and-websites)
  quote: “Alabama Montgomery AL” (3 words).

## B13-0101 — Which city is home to the Taj Mahal?

Answer: Agra. Fun fact: It is a mausoleum made of white marble.

  quote: “An immense mausoleum of white marble, built in Agra between 1631 and 1648” (13 words).

  quote: “an ivory-white marble mausoleum on the right bank of the river Yamuna in Agra” (14 words).

  quote: “The Taj Mahal is located on the right bank of the Yamuna River” (13 words).

  quote: “on the right bank of the river Yamuna in Agra” (10 words).

## B13-0103 — Which Mughal emperor commissioned the Taj Mahal?

Answer: Shah Jahan. Fun fact: It was built in memory of his wife Mumtaz Mahal.

## B13-0104 — What material gives the Taj Mahal’s main mausoleum its pale appearance?

Answer: White marble. Fun fact: The main building is a mausoleum, housing tombs.

  quote: “An immense mausoleum of white marble” (6 words).
- world-geography-s0002: [Wikipedia contributors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taj_Mahal)

## B13-0105 — The Great Wall defended ancient China mainly along which compass edge?

Answer: Northern. Fun fact: It formed a system of defensive fortifications.

- world-geography-s0003: [UNESCO World Heritage Centre](https://whc.unesco.org/en/list/438/)
  quote: “on the northern border of the country as the great military defence project” (13 words).
- world-geography-s0004: [Wikipedia contributors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Wall_of_China)
  quote: “They were built across the historical northern borders of ancient Chinese states and Imperial China as protection” (17 words).

## B13-0106 — Great Wall construction continued up to which dynasty, ruling China from 1368 to 1644?

Answer: Ming. Fun fact: The wall system was expanded under successive dynasties.

- world-geography-s0003: [UNESCO World Heritage Centre](https://whc.unesco.org/en/list/438/)
  quote: “Construction continued up to the Ming dynasty (1368–1644)” (8 words).
  funFactQuote: “the great military defence project of successive Chinese Empires” (9 words).
- world-geography-s0004: [Wikipedia contributors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Wall_of_China)
  quote: “Successive dynasties expanded the wall system; the best-known sections were built by the Ming dynasty (1368–1644).” (16 words).

## B13-0107 — Which first emperor joined earlier Chinese defensive walls into a unified system?

Answer: Qin Shi Huang. Fun fact: The Great Wall incorporated earlier fortifications.

- world-geography-s0003: [UNESCO World Heritage Centre](https://whc.unesco.org/en/list/438/)
  quote: “under Qin Shi Huang, sections of earlier fortifications were joined together to form a united defence system” (17 words).
- world-geography-s0004: [Wikipedia contributors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Wall_of_China)
  quote: “Shihuangdi (Qin Shi Huang), the first emperor of a united China (under the Qin dynasty), connected a number of existing defensive walls” (22 words).

## B13-0108 — Which Great Wall structures were specifically used as elevated lookout posts?

Answer: Watchtowers. Fun fact: The wall also included shelters or stations for defenders.

- world-geography-s0003: [UNESCO World Heritage Centre](https://whc.unesco.org/en/list/438/)
  quote: “Its main body consists of walls, horse tracks, watch towers, and shelters on the wall” (15 words).
- world-geography-s0004: [Wikipedia contributors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Wall_of_China)
  quote: “the Great Wall utilized watchtowers , troop barracks, garrison stations” (10 words).

## B13-0112 — The ancient city of Petra was the capital of which kingdom?

Answer: Nabataean. Fun fact: Its caravan trade linked regional routes.

  quote: “Petra would later become the capital city of the Nabataean Kingdom” (11 words).

## B13-0113 — Petra’s famous facades within sandstone cliffs were created by doing what to the rock?

Answer: Carving into it. Fun fact: The city’s landscape includes sandstone.

  funFactQuote: “set in a dominating red sandstone landscape” (7 words).
- world-geography-s0008: [Wikipedia contributors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petra)
  quote: “Famous for its rock-cut architecture” (5 words).
  funFactQuote: “because of the colour of the sandstone from which it is carved.” (12 words).

## B13-0114 — Which rock gives Petra’s landscape its characteristic reddish tone?

Answer: Sandstone. Fun fact: Its famous structures include rock-cut architecture.

- world-geography-s0007: [UNESCO World Heritage Centre](https://whc.unesco.org/en/list/326/)
  quote: “set in a dominating red sandstone landscape” (7 words).
  funFactQuote: “Petra is half-built, half-carved into the rock” (7 words).
- world-geography-s0008: [Wikipedia contributors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petra)

## B13-0115 — Which engineering achievement helped Petra support settlement in an arid landscape?

Answer: Water management. Fun fact: Channels carried water around the settlement.

  quote: “The Nabataeans were able to survive the desert due to efficient rainwater collecting” (13 words).

## B13-0128 — Which architect designed the Acropolis gateway known as the Propylaea?

Answer: Mnesicles. Fun fact: The structure formed the monumental entrance.

## B13-0129 — Rome is the capital of which country?

## B13-0130 — Which river runs through Rome?

## B13-0131 — What year does Roman legend traditionally give for Rome’s founding?

Answer: 753 BC. Fun fact: This is a legendary date, rather than proof of the first human settlement.

Answer: Romulus and Remus. Fun fact: The story is presented as myth, rather than established archaeology.

## B13-0133 — Stonehenge stands in which English county?

Answer: Wiltshire. Fun fact: Its setting contains prehistoric stone circles.

## B13-0134 — Stonehenge’s famous monument is notably aligned with sunrise and sunset around which annual events?

## B13-0135 — What is the name for a horizontal stone resting across two upright supports at Stonehenge?

Answer: Lintel. Fun fact: Horizontal stones cap parts of its circles and trilithons.

  quote: “two bulkier vertical sarsens joined by a single lintel” (9 words).

Answer: Sarsen and bluestone. Fun fact: The monument includes a prehistoric stone circle.

## B13-0173 — Which mountain has the highest summit above sea level on Earth?

Answer: Mount Everest. Fun fact: It is also known in Nepali as Sagarmatha.

## B13-0174 — Mount Everest belongs to which mountain range?

## B13-0175 — Sagarmatha National Park, protecting the southern side of Everest, is in which country?

  funFactQuote: “alpine vegetation extends to the feet of the glaciers” (9 words).

## B13-0176 — What Nepali name is used for Mount Everest?

Answer: Sagarmatha. Fun fact: Everest is the world’s highest summit above sea level.

## B13-0177 — Lake Baikal is in which country?

Answer: Russia. Fun fact: It lies in Siberia.

  quote: “Known as the 'Galapagos of Russia'” (6 words).

- world-geography-s0040: [Wikipedia contributors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Baikal)

## B13-0178 — Which lake is recognized as the world’s deepest?

Answer: Lake Baikal. Fun fact: It contains freshwater.

- world-geography-s0039: [UNESCO World Heritage Centre](https://whc.unesco.org/en/list/754/)
  quote: “deepest (1,700 m) lake in the world” (7 words).

- world-geography-s0040: [Wikipedia contributors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Baikal)
  quote: “Lake Baikal [ a ] is a rift lake and the deepest lake in the world.” (16 words).
  funFactQuote: “the world's largest freshwater lake by volume” (7 words).

## B13-0179 — Which Siberian lake is widely described as the world’s oldest existing lake?

Answer: Lake Baikal. Fun fact: Many of its species are endemic to the region.

- world-geography-s0039: [UNESCO World Heritage Centre](https://whc.unesco.org/en/list/754/)
  quote: “Lake Baikal is the oldest” (5 words).

  funFactQuote: “many of them endemic to the region” (7 words).

## B13-0180 — Lake Baikal is primarily which kind of lake by water salinity?

Answer: Freshwater. Fun fact: It is a vast inland freshwater reservoir.

  quote: “the world's total unfrozen freshwater reserve” (6 words).
- world-geography-s0040: [Wikipedia contributors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Baikal)

## B13-0181 — Pamukkale’s famous mineral terraces are in which country?

Answer: Turkey. Fun fact: The site is associated with thermal spring water.

- world-geography-s0041: [UNESCO World Heritage Centre](https://whc.unesco.org/en/list/485/)
  quote: “the plain of Cürüksu in south-west Turkey” (7 words).
  funFactQuote: “Its hot springs” (3 words).
- world-geography-s0071: [Encyclopaedia Britannica](https://www.britannica.com/place/Denizli)
  quote: “Denizli , city, southwestern Turkey” (5 words).
  funFactQuote: “North of Denizli is Pamukkale (ancient Hierapolis ), a resort around which limestone deposits from the hot springs” (18 words).

## B13-0182 — Which ancient spa city stands beside Pamukkale’s mineral terraces?

Answer: Hierapolis. Fun fact: The archaeological remains include baths and temples.

- world-geography-s0041: [UNESCO World Heritage Centre](https://whc.unesco.org/en/list/485/)
  quote: “established the thermal spa of Hierapolis. The ruins of the baths, temples” (12 words).
  funFactQuote: “at Pamukkale” (2 words).
- world-geography-s0066: [Go Türkiye](https://denizli.goturkiye.com/see)
  quote: “Hierapolis, an ancient spa city in the Roman and Byzantine periods” (11 words).
  funFactQuote: “In Hierapolis, you can see baths, temples and ruins of ancient theater.” (12 words).

## B13-0183 — Pamukkale’s terraces are mainly formed by deposits of which mineral compound?

Answer: Calcium carbonate. Fun fact: The resulting deposits form travertine.

- world-geography-s0041: [UNESCO World Heritage Centre](https://whc.unesco.org/en/list/485/)
  quote: “Fresh deposits of calcium carbonate give these formations a dazzling white coating.” (12 words).
  funFactQuote: “the white travertine terraces and numerous thermal springs” (8 words).
- world-geography-s0042: [Wikipedia contributors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pamukkale)
  quote: “calcium carbonate is deposited” (4 words).
  funFactQuote: “Calcium carbonate is deposited by the water as a soft gel which eventually crystallizes into travertine.” (16 words).

## B13-0184 — What color is characteristic of Pamukkale’s mineral terraces?

Answer: White. Fun fact: The pale terraces contain mineral-rich spring water.

- world-geography-s0041: [UNESCO World Heritage Centre](https://whc.unesco.org/en/list/485/)
  quote: “Fresh deposits of calcium carbonate give these formations a dazzling white coating.” (12 words).
- world-geography-s0066: [Go Türkiye](https://denizli.goturkiye.com/see)
  quote: “Unlike the white travertines in Pamukkale” (6 words).
  funFactQuote: “calcite travertine filled with warm, mineral-rich waters” (7 words).

## B13-0185 — The Cappadocia region with Göreme’s rock-cut sites is in which country?

Answer: Turkey. Fun fact: Erosion has sculpted much of its unusual rocky landscape.

- world-geography-s0043: [UNESCO World Heritage Centre](https://whc.unesco.org/en/list/357/)
  quote: “Türkiye Date of Inscription” (4 words).

- world-geography-s0054: [Encyclopaedia Britannica](https://www.britannica.com/place/Cappadocia)
  quote: “in the centre of present-day Turkey” (6 words).
  funFactQuote: “soft volcanic rock , shaped by erosion into towers, cones, valleys, and caves” (13 words).

## B13-0186 — Which natural process sculpted Cappadocia’s soft volcanic rock into towers and cones?

Answer: Erosion. Fun fact: The region also contains carved churches and underground complexes.

- world-geography-s0043: [UNESCO World Heritage Centre](https://whc.unesco.org/en/list/357/)
  quote: “a volcanic landscape sculpted by erosion” (6 words).
  funFactQuote: “rock-hewn cells, churches, troglodyte villages and subterranean cities” (8 words).
- world-geography-s0054: [Encyclopaedia Britannica](https://www.britannica.com/place/Cappadocia)
  quote: “soft volcanic rock , shaped by erosion into towers, cones, valleys, and caves.” (13 words).
  funFactQuote: “Rock-cut churches and underground tunnel complexes” (6 words).

## B13-0187 — Which historical era is associated with Cappadocia’s rock-cut churches?

Answer: Byzantine. Fun fact: The churches were carved into the rocky landscape.

- world-geography-s0043: [UNESCO World Heritage Centre](https://whc.unesco.org/en/list/357/)
  quote: “rock-hewn sanctuaries that provide unique evidence of Byzantine art” (9 words).
- world-geography-s0054: [Encyclopaedia Britannica](https://www.britannica.com/place/Cappadocia)
  quote: “Rock-cut churches and underground tunnel complexes from the Byzantine and Islamic eras” (12 words).

## B13-0188 — Cappadocia’s Derinkuyu and Kaymaklı are famous primarily as what?

Answer: Underground cities. Fun fact: They form part of the region’s cave and tunnel heritage.

  quote: “the subterranean cities of Kaymaklı and Derinkuyu.” (7 words).

  quote: “the sprawling underground cites of Derinkuyu and Kaymaklı” (8 words).

## B13-0189 — Venice is a famous lagoon city in which country?

## B13-0190 — Venice lies in which Italian region?

Answer: Veneto. Fun fact: Venice was historically a major maritime power.

- world-geography-s0045: [UNESCO World Heritage Centre](https://whc.unesco.org/en/list/394/)

## B13-0191 — What kind of water feature surrounds Venice’s historic island city?

Answer: A coastal lagoon. Fun fact: Canals also divide and connect parts of the city.

- world-geography-s0045: [UNESCO World Heritage Centre](https://whc.unesco.org/en/list/394/)
  quote: “Venice and its lagoon” (4 words).

  quote: “The islands are in the shallow Venetian Lagoon , an enclosed bay” (12 words).
  funFactQuote: “separated by expanses of open water and by canals” (9 words).

## B13-0192 — Medieval Venice was especially influential as what kind of power?

Answer: Maritime trading power. Fun fact: Its commerce linked it with other Mediterranean powers.

- world-geography-s0045: [UNESCO World Heritage Centre](https://whc.unesco.org/en/list/394/)
  quote: “Venice became a major maritime power” (6 words).
  funFactQuote: “defend its trading markets against the commercial undertakings of the Arabs, the Genoese and the Ottoman Turks” (17 words).
- world-geography-s0046: [Wikipedia contributors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venice)
  quote: “a major financial and maritime power during the Middle Ages and Renaissance” (12 words).
  funFactQuote: “a flourishing trade centre between Western Europe and the rest of the world” (13 words).

## B13-0193 — Plitvice Lakes National Park is in which country?

Answer: Croatia. Fun fact: Its lakes cascade from one into the next.

- world-geography-s0049: [UNESCO World Heritage Centre](https://whc.unesco.org/en/list/98/)
  quote: “Plitvice Lakes National Park, Croatia's largest national park” (8 words).
  funFactQuote: “Interconnected by many waterfalls and watercourses” (6 words).
- world-geography-s0072: [Plitvice Lakes National Park](https://np-plitvicka-jezera.hr/en/about-the-park/general-information/)
  quote: “Plitvice Lakes is the oldest and largest national park in the Republic of Croatia.” (14 words).
  funFactQuote: “several smaller unnamed lakes, cascading one into the next” (9 words).

## B13-0194 — What mineral-built natural barriers help form the Plitvice lake system?

Answer: Tufa or travertine barriers. Fun fact: These barriers slow or hold flowing water.

- world-geography-s0049: [UNESCO World Heritage Centre](https://whc.unesco.org/en/list/98/)
  quote: “deposited travertine barriers, creating natural dams” (6 words).
- world-geography-s0050: [Wikipedia contributors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plitvice_Lakes_National_Park)
  quote: “The emerging travertine barriers decelerated and retained the flowing water.” (10 words).

## B13-0195 — Which landscape term describes the geological setting of Plitvice Lakes?

Answer: Karst. Fun fact: Some of its lakes lie in a limestone canyon.

- world-geography-s0049: [UNESCO World Heritage Centre](https://whc.unesco.org/en/list/98/)
  quote: “Within a beautiful karst landscape” (5 words).
  funFactQuote: “smaller and shallower, are situated in limestone canyon” (8 words).
- world-geography-s0075: [Plitvice Lakes National Park](https://np-plitvicka-jezera.hr/en/natural-and-cultural-heritage/natural-heritage/karst-landscape/)
  quote: “The Plitvice Lakes National Park area is part of the Dinarides karst region” (13 words).
  funFactQuote: “The Lower lakes are formed in the narrow limestone canyon with steep banks” (13 words).

## B13-0196 — Which pair of large wild mammals occurs in Plitvice Lakes National Park?

Answer: Bears and wolves. Fun fact: These animals are among the park’s notable fauna.

- world-geography-s0049: [UNESCO World Heritage Centre](https://whc.unesco.org/en/list/98/)
  quote: “The forests in the park are home to bears, wolves” (10 words).
- world-geography-s0050: [Wikipedia contributors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plitvice_Lakes_National_Park)
  quote: “home to the European brown bear , grey wolf” (9 words).

## B13-0198 — Which of these countries contains a large part of the Amazon rainforest?

## B13-0199 — The Amazon River basin extends westward from the Atlantic toward which mountain range?
