- science-space-s0005: [National Institute of Standards and Technology](https://www.nist.gov/pml/special-publication-811/nist-guide-si-chapter-4-two-classes-si-units-and-si-prefixes)
  quote: “magnetic flux density tesla T” (5 words).

## B13-0272 — Which named SI derived unit measures magnetic flux?

Answer: weber. Fun fact: The symbol for the weber is Wb.

- science-space-s0003: [Wikipedia contributors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SI_derived_unit)
  quote: “weber Wb magnetic flux” (4 words).
- science-space-s0005: [National Institute of Standards and Technology](https://www.nist.gov/pml/special-publication-811/nist-guide-si-chapter-4-two-classes-si-units-and-si-prefixes)
  quote: “magnetic flux weber Wb” (4 words).

## B13-0273 — Which named SI derived unit expresses temperature relative to 273.15 K?

Answer: degree Celsius. Fun fact: The symbol for the degree Celsius is °C.

- science-space-s5003: [Wikipedia contributors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SI_derived_unit)
  quote: “degree Celsius °C temperature relative to 273.15 K” (8 words).
- science-space-s5005: [National Institute of Standards and Technology](https://www.nist.gov/pml/special-publication-811/nist-guide-si-chapter-4-two-classes-si-units-and-si-prefixes)
  quote: “Celsius temperature degree Celsius (f) °C” (6 words).
  extraQuote: “where T 0 = 273.15 K by definition.” (8 words).

## B13-0274 — Which named SI derived unit measures luminous flux?

Answer: lumen. Fun fact: The symbol for the lumen is lm.

- science-space-s0003: [Wikipedia contributors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SI_derived_unit)
  quote: “lumen lm luminous flux” (4 words).
- science-space-s0005: [National Institute of Standards and Technology](https://www.nist.gov/pml/special-publication-811/nist-guide-si-chapter-4-two-classes-si-units-and-si-prefixes)
  quote: “luminous flux lumen lm” (4 words).

## B13-0275 — Which named SI derived unit measures illuminance?

Answer: lux. Fun fact: The symbol for the lux is lx.

- science-space-s0003: [Wikipedia contributors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SI_derived_unit)
  quote: “lux lx illuminance” (3 words).
- science-space-s0005: [National Institute of Standards and Technology](https://www.nist.gov/pml/special-publication-811/nist-guide-si-chapter-4-two-classes-si-units-and-si-prefixes)
  quote: “illuminance lux lx” (3 words).

## B13-0276 — Which named SI derived unit measures radioactivity expressed as decays per unit time?

Answer: becquerel. Fun fact: The symbol for the becquerel is Bq.

- science-space-s0003: [Wikipedia contributors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SI_derived_unit)
  quote: “becquerel Bq radioactivity (decays per unit time)” (7 words).
- science-space-s0005: [National Institute of Standards and Technology](https://www.nist.gov/pml/special-publication-811/nist-guide-si-chapter-4-two-classes-si-units-and-si-prefixes)
  quote: “activity referred to a radionuclide (g) becquerel (d) Bq” (9 words).

## B13-0277 — Which named SI derived unit measures absorbed dose of ionizing radiation?

Answer: gray. Fun fact: The symbol for the gray is Gy.

- science-space-s0003: [Wikipedia contributors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SI_derived_unit)
  quote: “gray Gy absorbed dose (of ionizing radiation )” (8 words).
- science-space-s0005: [National Institute of Standards and Technology](https://www.nist.gov/pml/special-publication-811/nist-guide-si-chapter-4-two-classes-si-units-and-si-prefixes)
  quote: “absorbed dose, specific energy (imparted), kerma gray Gy” (8 words).

## B13-0278 — Which named SI derived unit measures equivalent dose of ionizing radiation?

Answer: sievert. Fun fact: The symbol for the sievert is Sv.

- science-space-s0003: [Wikipedia contributors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SI_derived_unit)
  quote: “sievert Sv equivalent dose (of ionizing radiation )” (8 words).
- science-space-s0005: [National Institute of Standards and Technology](https://www.nist.gov/pml/special-publication-811/nist-guide-si-chapter-4-two-classes-si-units-and-si-prefixes)
  quote: “dose equivalent, ambient dose equivalent, directional dose equivalent, personal dose equivalent sievert (h) Sv” (14 words).

## B13-0279 — Which named SI derived unit measures catalytic activity?

Answer: katal. Fun fact: The symbol for the katal is kat.

- science-space-s0003: [Wikipedia contributors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SI_derived_unit)
  quote: “katal kat catalytic activity” (4 words).
- science-space-s0005: [National Institute of Standards and Technology](https://www.nist.gov/pml/special-publication-811/nist-guide-si-chapter-4-two-classes-si-units-and-si-prefixes)
  quote: “catalytic activity katal kat” (4 words).

## B13-0280 — How many major planets orbit the Sun in our solar system?

Answer: Eight. Fun fact: These planets all revolve around the Sun.

- science-space-s0006: [NASA Science](https://science.nasa.gov/solar-system/solar-system-facts/)
  quote: “Our solar system includes the Sun, eight planets” (8 words).
  funFactQuote: “the planets that orbit the Sun.” (6 words).
- science-space-s0007: [OpenStax, Rice University](https://openstax.org/books/astronomy-2e/pages/7-1-overview-of-our-planetary-system)
  quote: “The eight planets all revolve in the same direction around the Sun.” (12 words).

## B13-0281 — What kind of astronomical object is the Sun?

Answer: A star. Fun fact: The Sun holds more than 99 percent of the solar system’s mass.

- science-space-s0006: [NASA Science](https://science.nasa.gov/solar-system/solar-system-facts/)
  quote: “Our solar system has one star, eight planets” (8 words).
  funFactQuote: “our Sun was born, and it eventually amassed more than 99% of the available matter.” (15 words).
- science-space-s0007: [OpenStax, Rice University](https://openstax.org/books/astronomy-2e/pages/7-1-overview-of-our-planetary-system)
  quote: “The Sun, a star that is brighter than about 80% of the stars in the Galaxy” (16 words).
  funFactQuote: “Sun 99.80 Jupiter 0.10” (4 words).

## B13-0282 — Which planet is closest to the Sun?

Answer: Mercury. Fun fact: Mercury is also the smallest of the eight major planets.

- science-space-s0008: [NASA Science](https://science.nasa.gov/mercury/facts/)
  quote: “Mercury is the smallest planet in our solar system and nearest to the Sun.” (14 words).
- science-space-s0009: [OpenStax, Rice University](https://openstax.org/books/astronomy-2e/pages/9-5-mercury)
  quote: “Mercury is the nearest planet to the Sun” (8 words).
  funFactQuote: “Mercury is the smallest planet (except for the dwarf planets)” (10 words).

## B13-0283 — Which of the eight major planets is the smallest?

Answer: Mercury. Fun fact: Mercury completes an orbit of the Sun in about 88 Earth days.

- science-space-s0008: [NASA Science](https://science.nasa.gov/mercury/facts/)
  quote: “Mercury is the smallest planet in our solar system” (9 words).
  funFactQuote: “zipping around the Sun every 88 Earth days.” (8 words).
- science-space-s0009: [OpenStax, Rice University](https://openstax.org/books/astronomy-2e/pages/9-5-mercury)
  quote: “Mercury is the smallest planet (except for the dwarf planets)” (10 words).
  funFactQuote: “it has the shortest period of revolution about the Sun (88 of our days)” (14 words).

## B13-0284 — On which planet is one sidereal rotation longer than one orbit around the Sun?

Answer: Venus. Fun fact: Venus rotates in the opposite direction from Earth.

- science-space-s0010: [NASA Science](https://science.nasa.gov/venus/facts/)
  quote: “your “day” would be 243 Earth days long — longer even than a Venus year” (15 words).
  funFactQuote: “Venus spins backward compared to Earth.” (6 words).
- science-space-s0011: [OpenStax, Rice University](https://openstax.org/books/astronomy-2e/pages/10-1-the-nearest-planets-an-overview)
  quote: “The planet takes 225 Earth days to orbit the Sun and 243 Earth days to spin on its axis.” (19 words).
  funFactQuote: “it spins in a backward or retrograde direction (east to west).” (11 words).

## B13-0285 — Which gas makes up most of Venus’s atmosphere?

Answer: Carbon dioxide. Fun fact: Venus’s dense clouds contain sulfuric acid.

- science-space-s0010: [NASA Science](https://science.nasa.gov/venus/facts/)
  quote: “The atmosphere is mostly carbon dioxide” (6 words).
  funFactQuote: “with dense clouds composed of sulfuric acid.” (7 words).
- science-space-s0012: [OpenStax, Rice University](https://openstax.org/books/astronomy-2e/pages/10-3-the-massive-atmosphere-of-venus)
  quote: “The most abundant gas on Venus is carbon dioxide (CO 2 )” (12 words).
  funFactQuote: “a thick cloud layer is composed primarily of sulfuric acid droplets.” (11 words).

## B13-0286 — What acid forms most of the droplets in Venus’s thick cloud layer?

Answer: Sulfuric acid. Fun fact: Carbon dioxide is the main gas in the planet’s atmosphere.

- science-space-s0010: [NASA Science](https://science.nasa.gov/venus/facts/)
  quote: “with dense clouds composed of sulfuric acid.” (7 words).
  funFactQuote: “The atmosphere is mostly carbon dioxide” (6 words).
- science-space-s0012: [OpenStax, Rice University](https://openstax.org/books/astronomy-2e/pages/10-3-the-massive-atmosphere-of-venus)
  quote: “a thick cloud layer is composed primarily of sulfuric acid droplets.” (11 words).
  funFactQuote: “The most abundant gas on Venus is carbon dioxide (CO 2 )” (12 words).

## B13-0287 — Which pair of planets are called the gas giants?

Answer: Jupiter and Saturn. Fun fact: Both lie in the outer solar system.

- science-space-s0006: [NASA Science](https://science.nasa.gov/solar-system/solar-system-facts/)
  quote: “that is where we find gas giants Jupiter and Saturn” (10 words).
  funFactQuote: “materials we are used to seeing as ice, liquid, or gas settled in the outer regions of the young solar system.” (21 words).
- science-space-s0013: [OpenStax, Rice University](https://openstax.org/books/astronomy-2e/pages/11-1-exploring-the-outer-planets)
  quote: “In the outer solar system, gases dominate the two largest planets, Jupiter and Saturn , hence their nickname “gas giants.”” (20 words).

## B13-0288 — Which pair of planets are called the ice giants?

Answer: Uranus and Neptune. Fun fact: The interiors of ice giants include materials such as water, methane and ammonia.

- science-space-s0014: [NASA Science](https://science.nasa.gov/neptune/facts/)
  quote: “Neptune is one of two ice giants in the outer solar system (the other is Uranus).” (16 words).
  funFactQuote: “a hot dense fluid of "icy" materials – water, methane, and ammonia – above a small, rocky core.” (18 words).
- science-space-s0013: [OpenStax, Rice University](https://openstax.org/books/astronomy-2e/pages/11-1-exploring-the-outer-planets)
  quote: “Uranus and Neptune are sometimes called “ice giants”” (8 words).
  funFactQuote: “Common ices are water, methane, and ammonia” (7 words).

## B13-0289 — Which planet is the largest in our solar system?

Answer: Jupiter. Fun fact: Its mass is greater than the masses of all the other planets combined.

- science-space-s0015: [NASA Science](https://science.nasa.gov/jupiter/facts/)
  quote: “It's the largest planet in our solar system” (8 words).
  funFactQuote: “ending up with more than twice the combined material of the other bodies in the solar system.” (17 words).
- science-space-s0007: [OpenStax, Rice University](https://openstax.org/books/astronomy-2e/pages/7-1-overview-of-our-planetary-system)
  quote: “the largest one, Jupiter , which is more massive than all the rest of the planets combined.” (17 words).

## B13-0290 — What is Jupiter’s famous long-lived giant atmospheric storm called?

Answer: The Great Red Spot. Fun fact: It is an atmospheric storm rather than a solid surface feature.

- science-space-s0015: [NASA Science](https://science.nasa.gov/jupiter/facts/)
  quote: “Jupiter’s iconic Great Red Spot is a giant storm” (9 words).
- science-space-s0016: [OpenStax, Rice University](https://openstax.org/books/astronomy-2e/pages/11-3-atmospheres-of-the-giant-planets)
  quote: “The largest and most famous of Jupiter’s storms is the Great Red Spot” (13 words).
  funFactQuote: “The giant storm has persisted in Jupiter’s atmosphere” (8 words).

## B13-0291 — Which giant planet has the lowest average density?

Answer: Saturn. Fun fact: Its average density is lower than that of water.

- science-space-s0017: [NASA Science](https://science.nasa.gov/saturn/facts/)
  quote: “Saturn is the only planet in our solar system with an average density that is less than water.” (18 words).
- science-space-s0018: [OpenStax, Rice University](https://openstax.org/books/astronomy-2e/pages/11-2-the-giant-planets)
  quote: “Saturn’s mass is 95 times that of Earth, and its average density is only 0.7 g/cm 3 —the lowest of any planet.” (22 words).
  funFactQuote: “Since this is less than the density of water, Saturn would be light enough to float.” (16 words).

## B13-0292 — About how many degrees is Uranus’s spin axis tilted from its orbital north direction?

Answer: 98 degrees. Fun fact: This extreme tilt makes Uranus appear to orbit on its side.

- science-space-s0019: [NASA Science](https://science.nasa.gov/uranus/facts/)
  quote: “a tilt of 97.77 degrees.” (5 words).
  funFactQuote: “This unique tilt makes Uranus appear to spin sideways” (9 words).
- science-space-s0018: [OpenStax, Rice University](https://openstax.org/books/astronomy-2e/pages/11-2-the-giant-planets)
  quote: “Uranus , which has a spin axis tilted by 98° with respect to the north direction.” (16 words).
  funFactQuote: “we can say that Uranus orbits on its side” (9 words).

## B13-0293 — Which trio is included among the “icy” interior materials of Uranus?

Answer: Water, methane and ammonia. Fun fact: Uranus is classified as an ice giant, like Neptune.

- science-space-s0019: [NASA Science](https://science.nasa.gov/uranus/facts/)
  quote: “a hot dense fluid of "icy" materials – water, methane, and ammonia – above a small rocky core.” (18 words).
  funFactQuote: “Uranus is one of two ice giants in the outer solar system (the other is Neptune).” (16 words).
- science-space-s0013: [OpenStax, Rice University](https://openstax.org/books/astronomy-2e/pages/11-1-exploring-the-outer-planets)
  quote: “Common ices are water, methane, and ammonia” (7 words).
  funFactQuote: “Uranus and Neptune are sometimes called “ice giants”” (8 words).

## B13-0294 — Which of the eight major planets is farthest from the Sun?

Answer: Neptune. Fun fact: Neptune takes about 165 Earth years to orbit the Sun.

- science-space-s0014: [NASA Science](https://science.nasa.gov/neptune/facts/)
  quote: “Neptune is the eighth and most distant planet in our solar system.” (12 words).
  funFactQuote: “Neptune makes a complete orbit around the Sun (a year in Neptunian time) in about 165 Earth years” (18 words).
- science-space-s0018: [OpenStax, Rice University](https://openstax.org/books/astronomy-2e/pages/11-2-the-giant-planets)
  quote: “Uranus 19.2 84.1 51,200 14 1.3 17.2 Neptune 30.0 164.8” (10 words).
  funFactQuote: “Neptune 30.0 164.8 49,500 17 1.6 16.1” (7 words).

## B13-0295 — On which planet is the volcano Olympus Mons?

Answer: Mars. Fun fact: Olympus Mons is an immense volcano.

- science-space-s0020: [NASA Science](https://science.nasa.gov/mars/facts/)
  quote: “Mars is home to the largest volcano in the solar system, Olympus Mons” (13 words).
  funFactQuote: “Olympus Mons” (2 words).
- science-space-s0021: [OpenStax, Rice University](https://openstax.org/books/astronomy-2e/pages/10-4-the-geology-of-mars)
  quote: “The most dramatic volcano on Mars is Olympus Mons (Mount Olympus)” (11 words).
  funFactQuote: “The volume of this immense volcano is nearly 100 times greater than that of Mauna Loa in Hawaii.” (18 words).

## B13-0296 — Valles Marineris is a vast canyon system on which planet?

Answer: Mars. Fun fact: This canyon system extends for thousands of kilometers.

- science-space-s0020: [NASA Science](https://science.nasa.gov/mars/facts/)
  quote: “A vast canyon system called Valles Marineris is long enough to stretch from California to New York . This Martian canyon” (21 words).
  funFactQuote: “extends about 2,400 miles (3,870 kilometers) in length” (8 words).
- science-space-s0021: [OpenStax, Rice University](https://openstax.org/books/astronomy-2e/pages/10-4-the-geology-of-mars)
  quote: “Among the most spectacular tectonic features on Mars are the canyons called the Valles Marineris” (15 words).
  funFactQuote: “They extend for about 5000 kilometers” (6 words).

## B13-0297 — In which year was Pluto discovered?

Answer: 1930. Fun fact: Pluto is classified as a dwarf planet.

- science-space-s0022: [NASA Science](https://science.nasa.gov/dwarf-planets/pluto/facts/)
  quote: “Discovered in 1930, Pluto” (4 words).
  funFactQuote: “Pluto was reclassified as a dwarf planet in 2006” (9 words).
- science-space-s0007: [OpenStax, Rice University](https://openstax.org/books/astronomy-2e/pages/7-1-overview-of-our-planetary-system)
  quote: “The first to be found, in 1930, was Pluto” (9 words).
  funFactQuote: “The largest TNOs are also classed as dwarf planets” (9 words).

## B13-0298 — What is the largest object in the main asteroid belt?

Answer: Ceres. Fun fact: Ceres is classified as a dwarf planet.

- science-space-s0023: [NASA Science](https://science.nasa.gov/dwarf-planets/ceres/facts/)
  quote: “Dwarf planet Ceres is the largest object in the asteroid belt between Mars and Jupiter” (15 words).
- science-space-s0007: [OpenStax, Rice University](https://openstax.org/books/astronomy-2e/pages/7-1-overview-of-our-planetary-system)
  quote: “The largest TNOs are also classed as dwarf planets, as is the largest asteroid, Ceres” (15 words).

## B13-0299 — Which list contains the four terrestrial planets?

Answer: Mercury, Venus, Earth and Mars. Fun fact: Terrestrial planets have solid, rocky surfaces.

- science-space-s0006: [NASA Science](https://science.nasa.gov/solar-system/solar-system-facts/)
  quote: “Mercury, Venus, Earth, and Mars – are terrestrial planets. They are all small with solid, rocky surfaces.” (17 words).
- science-space-s0007: [OpenStax, Rice University](https://openstax.org/books/astronomy-2e/pages/7-1-overview-of-our-planetary-system)
  quote: “The four planets closest to the Sun (Mercury through Mars) are called the inner or terrestrial planets” (17 words).
  funFactQuote: “The terrestrial planets are relatively small worlds, composed primarily of rock and metal. All of them have solid surfaces” (19 words).

## B13-0300 — What two elements make up most of Saturn?

Answer: Hydrogen and helium. Fun fact: Saturn’s average density is lower than that of water.

- science-space-s0017: [NASA Science](https://science.nasa.gov/saturn/facts/)
  quote: “Like fellow gas giant Jupiter, Saturn is a massive ball made mostly of hydrogen and helium.” (16 words).
  funFactQuote: “Saturn is the only planet in our solar system with an average density that is less than water.” (18 words).
- science-space-s0018: [OpenStax, Rice University](https://openstax.org/books/astronomy-2e/pages/11-2-the-giant-planets)
  quote: “the interiors of Jupiter and Saturn are composed primarily of hydrogen and helium.” (13 words).
  funFactQuote: “Since this is less than the density of water, Saturn would be light enough to float.” (16 words).

## B13-0301 — Giant pandas use their 'pseudo-thumb' to hold stalks of which plant?

Answer: Bamboo. Fun fact: An enlarged wrist bone helps pandas grip bamboo like a thumb.

- animals-nature-s0001: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/giant-panda)
  quote: “This posture leaves the front paws free to grasp bamboo stems with the help of a "pseudo thumb,"” (18 words).
  funFactQuote: “"pseudo thumb," formed by an elongated and enlarged wrist bone covered with a fleshy pad of skin.” (17 words).
- animals-nature-s0002: [San Diego Zoo Animals & Plants](https://animals.sandiegozoo.org/animals/giant-panda)
  quote: “They grasp bamboo stalks using their five digits and an enlarged wrist bone called a “pseudo-thumb.”” (16 words).

## B13-0302 — What is the usual name for a social group of lions?

Answer: A pride. Fun fact: Related females form the core of a lion pride.

- animals-nature-s0003: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/lion)
  quote: “They live in groups of related females, called prides” (9 words).
- animals-nature-s0004: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Panthera_leo/)
  quote: “Prides are comprised of related females.” (6 words).

## B13-0303 — Which adult lions typically have the conspicuous mane?

Answer: Males. Fun fact: A male lion’s mane can vary from blond to black.

- animals-nature-s0003: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/lion)
  quote: “adult males have shaggy manes that range in color from blond to reddish-brown to black.” (15 words).
- animals-nature-s0004: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Panthera_leo/)
  quote: “Three year-old male lions grow manes that vary in color from black to blond.” (14 words).

## B13-0304 — The Gir Forest’s wild Asiatic lions live in which country?

Answer: India. Fun fact: Their surviving native range is associated with northwestern India.

- animals-nature-s0003: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/lion)
  quote: “Gir Forest of northwest India” (5 words).
- animals-nature-s0004: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Panthera_leo/)
  quote: “Asiatic lions persist in the Gir forest of northwest India.” (10 words).

## B13-0305 — In lion prides, which adults do most of the hunting?

Answer: Females. Fun fact: Lion hunting can be a group activity.

- animals-nature-s0003: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/lion)
  quote: “Females raise the cubs and are the primary hunters, although males will sometimes join females during a hunt.” (18 words).
- animals-nature-s0004: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Panthera_leo/)
  quote: “Showy males have more difficulty hunting than females because of their conspicuousness, therefore females in a pride do the majority of hunting.” (22 words).
  funFactQuote: “They usually hunt in groups” (5 words).

## B13-0306 — Which animal is the fastest land mammal over a short sprint?

Answer: Cheetah. Fun fact: Its speed adaptations support very short sprints.

- animals-nature-s0005: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/cheetah)
  quote: “Cheetahs are aerodynamically built for speed and are the fastest land mammal.” (12 words).
  funFactQuote: “No other land mammal surpasses their short sprints.” (8 words).
- animals-nature-s0055: [San Diego Zoo Animals & Plants](https://animals.sandiegozoo.org/animals/cheetah)
  quote: “As the fastest land mammal, cheetahs have unique adaptations that help them run very fast for short distances.” (18 words).

## B13-0307 — Which cat has claws that remain partly exposed instead of retracting completely?

Answer: Cheetah. Fun fact: The claws help give it traction while running.

- animals-nature-s0005: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/cheetah)
  quote: “Special paw pads and semi-retractable claws provide great traction.” (9 words).
- animals-nature-s0055: [San Diego Zoo Animals & Plants](https://animals.sandiegozoo.org/animals/cheetah)
  quote: “A cheetah can retract its claws a bit” (8 words).
  funFactQuote: “Hard and sharp claws act like cleats, providing great traction when running.” (12 words).

## B13-0308 — A closely bonded group of adult male cheetahs is commonly called what?

Answer: A coalition. Fun fact: Brothers may remain together in one of these groups.

- animals-nature-s0005: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/cheetah)
  quote: “A coalition is usually made up of two to three littermates and is a very tightly bonded group.” (18 words).
- animals-nature-s0055: [San Diego Zoo Animals & Plants](https://animals.sandiegozoo.org/animals/cheetah)
  quote: “related males, usually brothers, live in small groups called coalitions.” (10 words).

## B13-0309 — Which cat’s black facial stripes are often nicknamed “tear marks”?

Answer: Cheetah. Fun fact: Cheetah cubs have a long-haired mantle along their backs.

- animals-nature-s0005: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/cheetah)
  quote: “A black tear mark runs from the inner corner of each eye down to the mouth.” (16 words).
  funFactQuote: “Cubs are smoky-grey in color with long hair, called a mantle, running along their backs.” (15 words).
- animals-nature-s0055: [San Diego Zoo Animals & Plants](https://animals.sandiegozoo.org/animals/cheetah)
  quote: “Cheetahs are the only cats with black “tear marks” on their face” (12 words).
  funFactQuote: “Cubs also have a stripe of long, silver fur called a mantle that runs down their back.” (17 words).

## B13-0310 — What is the characteristic dark coat pattern on a tiger?

Answer: Stripes. Fun fact: Tigers normally have pale or white undersides.

- animals-nature-s0007: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/tiger)
  quote: “Tigers have reddish-orange coats with prominent black stripes, white bellies and white spots on their ears.” (16 words).
- animals-nature-s0008: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Panthera_tigris/)
  quote: “vertical black stripes along the flanks and shoulders that vary in size, length, and spacing.” (15 words).
  funFactQuote: “The underside of the limbs and belly, chest, throat, and muzzle are white or light.” (15 words).

## B13-0311 — Outside mating and raising cubs, wild tigers generally live in which social pattern?

Answer: Solitary. Fun fact: A mother and her offspring are a major exception to that pattern.

- animals-nature-s0007: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/tiger)
  quote: “Tigers in the wild are thought to be solitary creatures, except when mating or raising young.” (16 words).
- animals-nature-s0008: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Panthera_tigris/)
  quote: “Tigers are solitary, the only long-term relationship is between a mother and her offspring.” (14 words).

## B13-0312 — Wild tigers are most active for hunting during which part of the day?

Answer: Night. Fun fact: They locate prey using sight and hearing.

- animals-nature-s5007: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/tiger)
  quote: “These powerful cats hunt primarily at night, using sight and sound to identify prey.” (14 words).
- animals-nature-s5008: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Panthera_tigris/)
  quote: “Tigers are most active at night” (6 words).
  funFactQuote: “Tigers are thought to locate their prey using hearing and sight more than olfaction” (14 words).

## B13-0313 — What color is the spot found on the back of a tiger’s ear?

Answer: White. Fun fact: Those pale ear marks contrast with the dark stripes on its body.

- animals-nature-s0007: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/tiger)
  quote: “with prominent black stripes, white bellies and white spots on their ears.” (12 words).
- animals-nature-s0008: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Panthera_tigris/)
  quote: “A white spot is present on the back of each ear.” (11 words).
  funFactQuote: “vertical black stripes along the flanks and shoulders” (8 words).

## B13-0314 — Meerkats belong to which animal family?

Answer: Mongoose. Fun fact: Their faces have conspicuous dark rings or patches around the eyes.

- animals-nature-s0009: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/meerkat)
  quote: “A member of the mongoose family, meerkats” (7 words).
  funFactQuote: “dark patches around their eyes” (5 words).
- animals-nature-s0010: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Suricata_suricatta/)
  quote: “Meerkats are one of the smallest species of mongooses in the family Herpestidae .” (14 words).
  funFactQuote: “Meerkats also have black rings around their large eyes.” (9 words).

## B13-0315 — What is a meerkat sentinel’s main role while its group forages?

Answer: Watches for danger. Fun fact: Members can take turns performing this lookout duty.

- animals-nature-s0009: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/meerkat)
  quote: “One member of the group will act as a sentinel, on the lookout for predators.” (15 words).
  funFactQuote: “Individuals rotate sentinel duty throughout the day.” (7 words).
- animals-nature-s0010: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Suricata_suricatta/)
  quote: “While mobs are foraging, one typically acts as sentinel and alarms others of potential dangers.” (15 words).
  funFactQuote: “This duty is shared between individuals within a mob” (9 words).

## B13-0316 — Which of these is a usual collective name for a group of meerkats?

Answer: A mob. Fun fact: Other group members can help parents care for young meerkats.

- animals-nature-s0009: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/meerkat)
  quote: “A group of meerkats is called a mob.” (8 words).
  funFactQuote: “non-breeding individuals in the pack also help to care for the young.” (12 words).
- animals-nature-s0010: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Suricata_suricatta/)
  quote: “They live in groups, called mobs or gangs.” (8 words).
  funFactQuote: “Other members of the social group, or mob, are also involved in rearing” (13 words).

## B13-0317 — Meerkats are described as diurnal. When are they normally active?

Answer: During the day. Fun fact: They spend much of their active time foraging.

- animals-nature-s0009: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/meerkat)
  quote: “Meerkats are diurnal and spend most of their active time foraging, basking in the sun, and grooming.” (17 words).
- animals-nature-s0010: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Suricata_suricatta/)
  quote: “Meerkats are diurnal and thus are frequently exposed to intense heat from the sun.” (14 words).
  funFactQuote: “Meerkats forage in groups for about 5 to 8 hours per day” (12 words).

## B13-0318 — A mature male gorilla is called a silverback because pale hair grows across which area?

Answer: Its back. Fun fact: The silverback name refers to age-related coat coloring.

- animals-nature-s0011: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/western-lowland-gorilla)
  quote: “Mature silverback males have a saddle of white hair across the small of their backs.” (15 words).
- animals-nature-s0012: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Gorilla_gorilla/)
  quote: “The dominant male, sometimes known as a silverback because of the age-related greying hair on his back” (17 words).

## B13-0319 — When a western gorilla walks on all fours, which part of its curled hand bears weight?

Answer: The knuckles. Fun fact: Its fingers are curled during this characteristic walk.

- animals-nature-s0011: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/western-lowland-gorilla)
  quote: “when they move on all fours, they knuckle-walk, supporting their weight on the third and fourth digits of their curled hands.” (21 words).
- animals-nature-s0012: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Gorilla_gorilla/)
  quote: “walk in a hunched, quadrupedal fashion, with hands curled and knuckles touching the ground.” (14 words).

## B13-0320 — Which food group supplies most of a wild western gorilla’s diet?

Answer: Plant material. Fun fact: Plant stems are among the foods it eats.

- animals-nature-s0011: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/western-lowland-gorilla)
  quote: “Gorillas are primarily herbivorous, eating the leaves and stems of herbs, shrubs and vines.” (14 words).
- animals-nature-s0012: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Gorilla_gorilla/)
  quote: “Wild gorillas are herbivores, subsisting mainly on juicy stemmed plants.” (10 words).

## B13-0321 — What materials do western gorillas arrange into their sleeping nests?

Answer: Branches and leaves. Fun fact: These nests may be built either on the ground or in trees.

- animals-nature-s0011: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/western-lowland-gorilla)
  quote: “pulling down and tucking branches, leaves or other vegetation around themselves.” (11 words).
  funFactQuote: “Females may nest on the ground or in trees.” (9 words).
- animals-nature-s0012: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Gorilla_gorilla/)
  quote: “Gorillas build day and night nests of branches and leaves for cushion on the ground or in trees.” (18 words).

## B13-0322 — What is the characteristic hair color of a Bornean orangutan?

Answer: Reddish. Fun fact: Its arms are very long.

- animals-nature-s0013: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/orangutan)
  quote: “Orangutans have long, sparse orange or reddish hair unequally distributed over their bodies.” (13 words).
  funFactQuote: “are very well adapted to life in the trees, with arms much longer than their legs.” (16 words).
- animals-nature-s0060: [Wikipedia contributors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bornean_orangutan)
  quote: “It has a coarse, reddish coat and up to 1.5 m (4 ft 11 in) long arms.” (17 words).

## B13-0323 — What name is used for the broad cheek pads of some adult male orangutans?

Answer: Flanges. Fun fact: Adult males can develop these conspicuous facial pads.

- animals-nature-s0013: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/orangutan)
  quote: “Flanged males (males with flanges, also known as cheek pads)” (10 words).
- animals-nature-s0060: [Wikipedia contributors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bornean_orangutan)
  quote: “It also has large, fatty cheek pads known as flanges” (10 words).

## B13-0324 — How often do orangutans usually build a fresh sleeping nest?

Answer: Each night. Fun fact: They sometimes reuse an older nest.

- animals-nature-s0013: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/orangutan)
  quote: “They usually build and sleep in a fresh nest each night” (11 words).
  funFactQuote: “they will sometimes re-enter or rebuild an older nest.” (9 words).
- animals-nature-s0063: [San Diego Zoo Animals & Plants](https://animals.sandiegozoo.org/animals/orangutan)
  quote: “They usually build a new nest every night, but may occasionally reuse one.” (13 words).

## B13-0325 — Which food is the main component of a Bornean orangutan’s diet?

Answer: Fruit. Fun fact: Flowers are also among their foods.

- animals-nature-s0013: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/orangutan)
  quote: “Orangutans eat primarily fruit and play an important role dispersing seeds through defecation.” (13 words).
  funFactQuote: “orangutans also eat insects and flowers” (6 words).
- animals-nature-s0063: [San Diego Zoo Animals & Plants](https://animals.sandiegozoo.org/animals/orangutan)
  quote: “Orangutans eat lots of ripe fruits—up to 100 kinds. Figs, along with fruit of the durian tree, are most common.” (20 words).
  funFactQuote: “they eat leaves, flowers, bark, honey, termites, ants and other insects, and even bird eggs.” (15 words).

## B13-0326 — How many fingerlike projections are at the tip of an Asian elephant’s trunk?

Answer: One. Fun fact: Elephant tusks are modified teeth of the upper jaw.

- animals-nature-s0015: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/asian-elephant)
  quote: “Asian elephants have one small projection at the end of their trunk” (12 words).
  funFactQuote: “Tusks are modified upper incisors” (5 words).
- animals-nature-s0016: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Elephas_maximus/)
  quote: “Asian elephants have one fingerlike projection at the tip of the trunk” (12 words).
  funFactQuote: “Males have tusks which are an elongation of the second upper incisors” (12 words).

## B13-0327 — Elephant tusks are elongated examples of which kind of tooth?

Answer: Incisors. Fun fact: They are modified teeth of the upper jaw.

- animals-nature-s0015: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/asian-elephant)
  quote: “Tusks are modified upper incisors that grow throughout an individual's life” (11 words).
- animals-nature-s0016: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Elephas_maximus/)
  quote: “Males have tusks which are an elongation of the second upper incisors” (12 words).

## B13-0328 — An Asian elephant’s wild diet is primarily composed of what?

Answer: Plants. Fun fact: Leaves are among its foods.

- animals-nature-s0015: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/asian-elephant)
  quote: “Asian elephants are herbivores” (4 words).
  funFactQuote: “including leaves, roots, vines, twigs, shoots and bark, as well as fruit.” (12 words).
- animals-nature-s0016: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Elephas_maximus/)
  quote: “Asian elephants are herbivores that eat many types of plant food.” (11 words).
  funFactQuote: “Plant Foods leaves roots and tubers wood, bark, or stems” (10 words).

## B13-0329 — Which listed animal typically has larger adult males than females?

Answer: Asian elephant. Fun fact: The species shows a noticeable size difference between the sexes.

- animals-nature-s0015: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/asian-elephant)
  quote: “Males are usually larger than females.” (6 words).
- animals-nature-s0016: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Elephas_maximus/)
  quote: “The males are much larger than the females.” (8 words).

## B13-0330 — Which plant supplies the main food eaten by a red panda?

Answer: Bamboo. Fun fact: Red pandas chiefly eat its leaves.

- animals-nature-s0017: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/red-panda)
  quote: “Bamboo constitutes about 95% of the red panda's diet.” (9 words).
  funFactQuote: “red pandas feed selectively on the most nutritious leaf tips” (10 words).
- animals-nature-s0018: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Ailurus_fulgens/)
  quote: “Bamboo (the leaves of which are the red panda's primary food source)” (12 words).

## B13-0331 — What is the main role of a red panda’s long tail while it is in a tree?

Answer: Keeping its balance. Fun fact: The tail has alternating red and buff rings.

- animals-nature-s0017: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/red-panda)
  quote: “Long, bushy tails help these arboreal animals maintain balance” (9 words).
  funFactQuote: “Their tails are marked with alternating red and buff rings.” (10 words).
- animals-nature-s0018: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Ailurus_fulgens/)
  quote: “The tail is used for balance when in trees” (9 words).
  funFactQuote: “The tails are marked with about 12 alternating red and buff rings” (12 words).

## B13-0332 — What pattern is visible on a red panda’s tail?

Answer: Alternating red and buff rings. Fun fact: Its tail is long and furry.

- animals-nature-s0017: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/red-panda)
  quote: “Their tails are marked with alternating red and buff rings.” (10 words).
  funFactQuote: “Long, bushy tails” (3 words).
- animals-nature-s0018: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Ailurus_fulgens/)
  quote: “The tails are marked with about 12 alternating red and buff rings” (12 words).
  funFactQuote: “relatively long, furry tails” (4 words).

## B13-0333 — What distinctive covering is found on the soles of a red panda’s feet?

Answer: Dense fur. Fun fact: Its feet have fur even on their undersides.

- animals-nature-s0017: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/red-panda)
  quote: “Dense fur completely covers their feet” (6 words).
- animals-nature-s0018: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Ailurus_fulgens/)
  quote: “the soles of its feet are covered with dense, white hair.” (11 words).

## B13-0334 — What supplies the pigments responsible for an American flamingo’s pink plumage?

Answer: Its food. Fun fact: The pink coloration depends on pigments in what the birds eat.

- animals-nature-s0023: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/american-flamingo)
  quote: “A pigment in their food gives their feathers their flamboyant pink color.” (12 words).
- animals-nature-s0064: [San Diego Zoo Animals & Plants](https://animals.sandiegozoo.org/animals/flamingo)
  quote: “color comes from the rich sources of carotenoid pigments (like the pigments of carrots) in the algae and small crustaceans the birds eat.” (23 words).

## B13-0335 — What color are an adult American flamingo’s flight feathers?

Answer: Black. Fun fact: These dark feathers contrast with its pink body plumage.

- animals-nature-s0023: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/american-flamingo)
  quote: “A flamingo's plumage is a distinctive pink color with black flight feathers along the edges of the wings.” (18 words).
- animals-nature-s0061: [Wikipedia contributors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_flamingo)
  quote: “the primary and secondary flight feathers are black.” (8 words).
  funFactQuote: “Most of its plumage is pink” (6 words).

## B13-0336 — What general shape does an American flamingo’s nest have?

Answer: A raised mound. Fun fact: Both partners help build the nest.

- animals-nature-s0023: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/american-flamingo)
  quote: “both parents help build a volcano-shaped nest” (7 words).
- animals-nature-s0064: [San Diego Zoo Animals & Plants](https://animals.sandiegozoo.org/animals/flamingo)
  quote: “A flamingo nest is not fancy, just a mound of mud” (11 words).
  funFactQuote: “Both the male and female build the nest” (8 words).

## B13-0337 — How many eggs does an American flamingo pair usually lay in one breeding cycle?

Answer: One. Fun fact: Both parents take part in incubation.

- animals-nature-s0023: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/american-flamingo)
  quote: “they lay a single egg that they then incubate” (9 words).
  funFactQuote: “both parents help build a volcano-shaped nest, where they lay a single egg that they then incubate for about 28 days.” (21 words).
- animals-nature-s0061: [Wikipedia contributors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_flamingo)
  quote: “it lays a single chalky-white egg on a mud mound” (10 words).
  funFactQuote: “The egg is attended constantly and equally by alternating parents.” (10 words).

## B13-0338 — Which species is the largest living bird?

Answer: Ostrich. Fun fact: Its native range includes Africa.

- animals-nature-s0025: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/ostrich)
  quote: “The world’s tallest and heaviest bird, the common ostrich is native to the grasslands and savannas of Africa.” (18 words).
- animals-nature-s0026: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Struthio_camelus/)
  quote: “Ostriches are the largest living birds.” (6 words).
  funFactQuote: “Ostriches are currently restricted to drier and sandy regions of central and southern Africa.” (14 words).

## B13-0339 — What is the typical adult male ostrich’s plumage color combination?

Answer: Black and white. Fun fact: Adult females are mainly brown or gray-brown.

- animals-nature-s0025: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/ostrich)
  quote: “adult males have black feathers and white tails, while females are mostly covered in brown feathers.” (16 words).
- animals-nature-s0026: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Struthio_camelus/)
  quote: “Males are black and white, females gray brown.” (8 words).

## B13-0340 — How do breeding ostriches often use an egg nest?

Answer: Several females share it. Fun fact: Both males and females help care for the eggs.

- animals-nature-s0025: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/ostrich)
  quote: “Ostrich nests are communal; several ostriches lay their eggs in the same nest.” (13 words).
  funFactQuote: “one male and one female take turns incubating the eggs.” (10 words).
- animals-nature-s0026: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Struthio_camelus/)
  quote: “ostriches in some areas use communal nests to hold anywhere from 15 to 60 eggs.” (15 words).
  funFactQuote: “Caring for their eggs is divided up between males and females.” (11 words).

## B13-0341 — The common ostrich’s surviving native wild range is on which continent?

Answer: Africa. Fun fact: Its habitats include relatively open, dry landscapes.

- animals-nature-s0025: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/ostrich)
  quote: “Common ostriches are mostly found in the grasslands, savanna and shrublands of southern and eastern Africa.” (16 words).
- animals-nature-s0026: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Struthio_camelus/)
  quote: “Ostriches are currently restricted to drier and sandy regions of central and southern Africa.” (14 words).

## B13-0342 — Emus are native to which country?

Answer: Australia. Fun fact: They occur across much of that continent.

- animals-nature-s0027: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/emu)
  quote: “Emus live only in Australia, where they are widespread.” (9 words).
- animals-nature-s0028: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Dromaius_novaehollandiae/)
  quote: “Emus ( Dromaius novaehollandiae ) are found exclusively in Australia and inhabits nearly the entire continent.” (16 words).

## B13-0343 — Which of these birds normally has the male incubate the eggs while the female may leave to mate again?

Answer: Emu. Fun fact: Females are generally somewhat larger than males.

- animals-nature-s0027: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/emu)
  quote: “The males incubate the eggs.” (5 words).
  funFactQuote: “females weigh about 11 pounds (5 kilograms) more than males.” (10 words).
  extraQuote: “Once the male starts sitting, most females leave the territory, sometimes pairing with other males and laying further clutches.” (19 words).
- animals-nature-s0028: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Dromaius_novaehollandiae/)
  quote: “After the males begin the incubation period” (7 words).
  funFactQuote: “with females being slightly by not significantly larger.” (8 words).
  extraQuote: “After the males begin the incubation period, the female emus will seek to mate with unoccupied males.” (17 words).

## B13-0344 — Which listed species has females that generally outweigh males?

Answer: Emu. Fun fact: Male emus perform the egg-incubation duty.

- animals-nature-s0027: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/emu)
  quote: “females weigh about 11 pounds (5 kilograms) more than males.” (10 words).
  funFactQuote: “The males incubate the eggs.” (5 words).
- animals-nature-s0028: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Dromaius_novaehollandiae/)
  quote: “with females being slightly by not significantly larger.” (8 words).
  funFactQuote: “After the males begin the incubation period” (7 words).

## B13-0345 — Emus belong to the ratites, a group best known for which trait?

Answer: Being flightless. Fun fact: Their long legs resemble those of other running ratites.

- animals-nature-s0027: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/emu)
  quote: “Emus are large, flightless birds resembling and related to ostriches.” (10 words).
  funFactQuote: “Their necks and legs are long” (6 words).
- animals-nature-s0028: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Dromaius_novaehollandiae/)
  quote: “Emus have long bare legs, similar to other flightless birds Ratites .” (12 words).

## B13-0346 — What is the helmet-like structure on a southern cassowary’s head called?

Answer: A casque. Fun fact: The structure is on top of the head.

- animals-nature-s0029: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/southern-cassowary)
  quote: “One of the cassowary’s most unique and dinosaur-like features is the casque atop its head.” (15 words).
- animals-nature-s0062: [Wikipedia contributors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_cassowary)
  quote: “A horn-like brown casque” (4 words).
  funFactQuote: “sits atop the head.” (4 words).

## B13-0347 — Southern cassowaries commonly forage for which fallen food in forests?

Answer: Fruit. Fun fact: It eats fruit in its forest habitat.

- animals-nature-s0029: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/southern-cassowary)
  quote: “cassowaries mostly eat fruits.” (4 words).
- animals-nature-s0062: [Wikipedia contributors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_cassowary)
  quote: “Southern cassowaries forage on the forest floor for fallen fruit and seeds” (12 words).

## B13-0348 — Which of these birds has the male incubate the eggs and then raise the chicks alone?

Answer: Southern cassowary. Fun fact: Female cassowaries are generally larger than males.

- animals-nature-s0029: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/southern-cassowary)
  quote: “Males are the sole caregivers, incubating the eggs by themselves and raising the chicks” (14 words).
  funFactQuote: “Females are slightly larger than males” (6 words).
- animals-nature-s0062: [Wikipedia contributors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_cassowary)
  quote: “The male also incubates the eggs and raises the chicks alone.” (11 words).
  funFactQuote: “the female is dominant and larger” (6 words).

## B13-0349 — Female-biased adult body size is characteristic of which animal here?

Answer: Southern cassowary. Fun fact: Males incubate the eggs.

- animals-nature-s0029: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/southern-cassowary)
  quote: “Females are slightly larger than males” (6 words).
  funFactQuote: “Males are the sole caregivers, incubating the eggs by themselves” (10 words).
- animals-nature-s0062: [Wikipedia contributors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_cassowary)
  quote: “the female is dominant and larger” (6 words).
  funFactQuote: “The male also incubates the eggs and raises the chicks alone.” (11 words).

## B13-0350 — Which insects are a staple food for sloth bears?

Answer: Termites. Fun fact: Their strong claws open termite nests.

- animals-nature-s0019: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/sloth-bear)
  quote: “Termites, dug out of their cement-hard nest mounds, and some ants are year-round staples.” (14 words).
  funFactQuote: “long, curved claws which they use to excavate termites and ants.” (11 words).
- animals-nature-s0020: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Melursus_ursinus/)
  quote: “feast on their prize by vacuuming the termites into their mouths.” (11 words).
  funFactQuote: “rip open the nest with their long claws” (8 words).

## B13-0351 — What can a sloth bear close to keep out dust while raiding an insect nest?

Answer: Its nostrils. Fun fact: Closing them helps prevent inhaling dust.

- animals-nature-s0019: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/sloth-bear)
  quote: “Sloth bears' nostrils can close completely, protecting the animals from dust or insects” (13 words).
- animals-nature-s0020: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Melursus_ursinus/)
  quote: “The ability to voluntarily open and close the nostrils prevents the inhalation of dust during this process.” (17 words).

## B13-0352 — Where do young sloth bear cubs often ride on their mother?

Answer: On her back. Fun fact: The cubs travel with their mother this way.

- animals-nature-s0019: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/sloth-bear)
  quote: “they routinely carry their cubs on their backs.” (8 words).
- animals-nature-s0020: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Melursus_ursinus/)
  quote: “Cubs often ride on the mother's back” (7 words).

## B13-0353 — Which body parts does a sloth bear use to rip open termite nests?

Answer: Its long claws. Fun fact: It then sucks termites into its mouth.

- animals-nature-s0019: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/sloth-bear)
  quote: “long, curved claws which they use to excavate termites and ants.” (11 words).
  funFactQuote: “which enables them to suck up termites and other insects with ease.” (12 words).
- animals-nature-s0020: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Melursus_ursinus/)
  quote: “rip open the nest with their long claws” (8 words).
  funFactQuote: “vacuuming the termites into their mouths.” (6 words).

## B13-0354 — Which teeth are unusually long in a clouded leopard?

Answer: Canines. Fun fact: This cat can climb headfirst down trees.

- animals-nature-s0021: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/clouded-leopard)
  quote: “Another distinctive feature of the clouded leopard is its long canine teeth.” (12 words).
  funFactQuote: “including climbing headfirst down trees.” (5 words).
- animals-nature-s0022: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Neofelis_nebulosa/)
  quote: “Clouded leopards have the longest canine teeth relative to head and body size” (13 words).
  funFactQuote: “climbing slowly head first down tree trunks” (7 words).

## B13-0355 — Which cat is notably adapted for headfirst descents of tree trunks?

Answer: Clouded leopard. Fun fact: It has short legs and a long tail.

- animals-nature-s0021: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/clouded-leopard)
  quote: “including climbing headfirst down trees.” (5 words).
  funFactQuote: “It has proportionately short legs and a long tail.” (9 words).
- animals-nature-s0022: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Neofelis_nebulosa/)
  quote: “climbing slowly head first down tree trunks” (7 words).
  funFactQuote: “Large feet, short legs, and a long tail make clouded leopards well-adapted for arboreal living.” (15 words).

## B13-0356 — What is a conspicuous feature of a clouded leopard’s coat?

Answer: Dark blotches. Fun fact: Its coat has a brownish or grayish background.

- animals-nature-s0021: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/clouded-leopard)
  quote: “The coat is brown or yellowish-gray and covered with irregular dark stripes, spots and blotches.” (15 words).
- animals-nature-s0022: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Neofelis_nebulosa/)
  quote: “The fur is marked with elliptical blotches of a darker color than the background” (14 words).
  funFactQuote: “a background field that varies from yellowish brown to dark gray.” (11 words).

## B13-0357 — A clouded leopard’s diet is primarily what type?

Answer: Carnivorous. Fun fact: It hunts vertebrates such as birds and mammals.

- animals-nature-s0021: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/clouded-leopard)
  quote: “Like all wild cats, clouded leopards are carnivores.” (8 words).
  funFactQuote: “They may also hunt birds and rodents.” (7 words).
- animals-nature-s0022: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Neofelis_nebulosa/)
  quote: “Like other felids, they are strict carnivores.” (7 words).
  funFactQuote: “preying on birds, fish, monkeys, deer, and rodents.” (8 words).

## B13-0358 — Which snout shape distinguishes the American alligator from many crocodiles?

Answer: Rounded at the tip. Fun fact: Males are generally larger than females.

- animals-nature-s0031: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/american-alligator)
  quote: “Alligators have a long, rounded snout” (6 words).
  funFactQuote: “Male alligators are larger than female alligators.” (7 words).
- animals-nature-s0032: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Alligator_mississippiensis/)
  quote: “the snout of the American alligator is round.” (8 words).
  funFactQuote: “a male adult alligator is larger than a female alligator.” (10 words).

## B13-0359 — What environmental factor helps determine an American alligator hatchling’s sex?

Answer: Incubation temperature. Fun fact: Sex determination occurs during egg development.

- animals-nature-s0031: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/american-alligator)
  quote: “The sex of the juveniles is determined by the temperature of the nest.” (13 words).
- animals-nature-s0032: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Alligator_mississippiensis/)
  quote: “The sex of the American alligator is temperature-determinant.” (8 words).

## B13-0360 — How is an American alligator’s egg nest normally prepared?

Answer: The female piles vegetation. Fun fact: The nest is made with plant material.

- animals-nature-s0031: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/american-alligator)
  quote: “After mating has taken place, the female builds a nest of vegetation.” (12 words).
- animals-nature-s0032: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Alligator_mississippiensis/)
  quote: “The female builds the nest using her tail” (8 words).
  funFactQuote: “The vegetation and other plant materials, which are used for shade, are formed to make a nest” (17 words).

## B13-0361 — Male-biased adult body size is typical of which animal here?

Answer: American alligator. Fun fact: This is a form of sexual dimorphism.

- animals-nature-s0031: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/american-alligator)
  quote: “Male alligators are larger than female alligators.” (7 words).
- animals-nature-s0032: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Alligator_mississippiensis/)
  quote: “as a characteristic of sexual dimorphism, a male adult alligator is larger than a female alligator.” (16 words).

## B13-0362 — The Aldabra giant tortoise is native to an atoll in which island nation?

Answer: Seychelles. Fun fact: Its native range includes Aldabra Atoll.

- animals-nature-s0033: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/aldabra-tortoise)
  quote: “They are native to Aldabra Island, one of the Seychelles northeast of Madagascar in the Indian Ocean.” (17 words).
  funFactQuote: “Aldabra Island is a coral atoll” (6 words).
- animals-nature-s0059: [Wikipedia contributors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aldabra_giant_tortoise)
  quote: “The species is endemic to the Seychelles” (7 words).
  funFactQuote: “A. g. gigantea native to Aldabra atoll.” (7 words).

## B13-0363 — What is the Aldabra giant tortoise’s diet mainly composed of?

Answer: Vegetation. Fun fact: Grasses and woody plant parts are among its foods.

- animals-nature-s0033: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/aldabra-tortoise)
  quote: “They are grazers and browsers, feeding mainly on grasses and woody plants.” (12 words).
- animals-nature-s0059: [Wikipedia contributors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aldabra_giant_tortoise)
  quote: “Primarily herbivores , Aldabra giant tortoises eat grasses , leaves , woody plant stems, and fruit.” (16 words).

## B13-0364 — Which body feature helps an Aldabra tortoise reach vegetation while browsing?

Answer: Its long neck. Fun fact: Its neck assists with food gathering.

- animals-nature-s0033: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/aldabra-tortoise)
  quote: “They have long necks, which helps with food gathering.” (9 words).
- animals-nature-s0059: [Wikipedia contributors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aldabra_giant_tortoise)
  quote: “The neck of the Aldabra giant tortoise is very long” (10 words).
  funFactQuote: “which helps the animal to exploit tree branches up to a meter from the ground as a food source.” (19 words).

## B13-0365 — What shape is an Aldabra giant tortoise’s upper shell?

Answer: Highly domed. Fun fact: This tortoise has a long neck.

- animals-nature-s0033: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/aldabra-tortoise)
  quote: “They are dark gray to black in color with a highly domed, thick carapace.” (14 words).
  funFactQuote: “They have long necks” (4 words).
- animals-nature-s0059: [Wikipedia contributors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aldabra_giant_tortoise)
  quote: “with a high, domed shape.” (5 words).
  funFactQuote: “The neck of the Aldabra giant tortoise is very long” (10 words).

## B13-0366 — Which fennec fox body feature helps dissipate heat?

Answer: Its large ears. Fun fact: Those ears also help detect prey beneath sand.

- animals-nature-s0035: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/fennec-fox)
  quote: “which they use to dispel heat and track down prey underneath the sand.” (13 words).
- animals-nature-s0036: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Vulpes_zerda/)
  quote: “the large, 15 cm long pinnae are used both to dissipate heat and to locate prey moving under the sand.” (20 words).

## B13-0367 — When does a fennec fox usually hunt?

Answer: At night. Fun fact: It shelters from the hot sun during the day.

- animals-nature-s0035: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/fennec-fox)
  quote: “Nocturnal animals, fennec foxes will hide out in burrows during daylight to avoid the hottest portion of the day.” (19 words).
- animals-nature-s0036: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Vulpes_zerda/)
  quote: “Nocturnal hunters, fennecs need places to sleep during the day in which they will be sheltered from the hot desert sun.” (21 words).

## B13-0368 — Fennec foxes are strongly associated with which desert?

Answer: Sahara. Fun fact: Their native range extends across parts of North Africa.

- animals-nature-s0035: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/fennec-fox)
  quote: “Fennec foxes live in North Africa, throughout the Sahara Desert” (10 words).
- animals-nature-s0036: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Vulpes_zerda/)
  quote: “The largest populations of Vulpes zerda occur in the central Sahara” (11 words).
  funFactQuote: “northern Morocco” (2 words).

## B13-0369 — What protects a fennec fox’s paws from hot desert sand?

Answer: Thick fur. Fun fact: The covering is present around the foot pads.

- animals-nature-s0035: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/fennec-fox)
  quote: “Heavily furred paws provide traction when running in the sand while also offering protection from the extreme heat of the terrain.” (21 words).
- animals-nature-s0036: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Vulpes_zerda/)
  quote: “The feet are heavily furred, protecting the pads from the hot desert sand.” (13 words).

## B13-0370 — What structures do North American beavers build to slow flowing water?

Answer: Dams. Fun fact: Slower water forms ponds around their habitat.

- animals-nature-s0037: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/beaver)
  quote: “Dams reduce stream erosion by forming slow-moving ponds.” (8 words).
- animals-nature-s0038: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Castor_canadensis/)
  quote: “Beavers build dams to slow down the flow of water in streams and rivers” (14 words).

## B13-0371 — What is the characteristic shape of a North American beaver’s tail?

Answer: Broad and flat. Fun fact: The tail is covered with scales.

- animals-nature-s0037: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/beaver)
  quote: “a broad, flat, scaly tail.” (5 words).
- animals-nature-s0038: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Castor_canadensis/)
  quote: “A beaver's tail is broad, flat, and covered with large black scales.” (12 words).

## B13-0372 — What is a North American beaver’s constructed home commonly called?

Answer: A lodge. Fun fact: Its living space is above the water level.

- animals-nature-s0037: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/beaver)
  quote: “Beavers also build dome-like lodges” (5 words).
  funFactQuote: “living quarters are located in the top of the lodge above the water line.” (14 words).
- animals-nature-s0038: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Castor_canadensis/)
  quote: “Beavers live in lodges” (4 words).
  funFactQuote: “The island lodge consists of a central chamber, with its floor slightly above the water level” (16 words).

## B13-0373 — How does a beaver use its tail to warn others of danger?

Answer: Slaps it on water. Fun fact: The slap creates a conspicuous warning signal.

- animals-nature-s0037: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/beaver)
  quote: “to signal danger when slapped on the water.” (8 words).
- animals-nature-s0038: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Castor_canadensis/)
  quote: “Beavers will also warn others of danger by slapping their tails against the water, creating a powerful noise.” (18 words).

## B13-0374 — Which insects make up most of a giant anteater’s food?

Answer: Ants and termites. Fun fact: Its sticky tongue collects this small prey.

- animals-nature-s0039: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/giant-anteater)
  quote: “Anteaters feed almost exclusively on ants and termites” (8 words).
  funFactQuote: “then ingest with their sticky tongue.” (6 words).
- animals-nature-s0040: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Myrmecophaga_tridactyla/)
  quote: “Giant anteaters eat ants, termites, and soft-bodied grubs.” (8 words).
  funFactQuote: “The salivary glands secrete sticky saliva during feeding that coats the tongue.” (12 words).

## B13-0375 — How many teeth does a giant anteater have?

Answer: None. Fun fact: It has a very long tongue instead of chewing teeth.

- animals-nature-s0039: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/giant-anteater)
  quote: “Giant anteaters have a long, distinctive snout with a 2-foot-long tongue and no teeth.” (14 words).
- animals-nature-s0040: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Myrmecophaga_tridactyla/)
  quote: “Giant anteaters have no teeth.” (5 words).
  funFactQuote: “The tongue can be extended 610 mm outside of the mouth” (11 words).

## B13-0376 — What helps prey stick to a giant anteater’s tongue?

Answer: Sticky saliva. Fun fact: The saliva coats the tongue during feeding.

- animals-nature-s0039: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/giant-anteater)
  quote: “When feeding, sticky saliva coats the tongue.” (7 words).
- animals-nature-s0040: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Myrmecophaga_tridactyla/)
  quote: “The salivary glands secrete sticky saliva during feeding that coats the tongue.” (12 words).

## B13-0377 — Why does a giant anteater walk on the knuckles of its front feet?

Answer: To protect its claws. Fun fact: Its front claws are used to open insect nests.

- animals-nature-s0039: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/giant-anteater)
  quote: “Giant anteaters protect their sharp front claws by tucking them into their palms and walking on their front knuckles.” (19 words).
  funFactQuote: “whose nests they rip open with their powerful forelimbs and claws” (11 words).
- animals-nature-s0040: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Myrmecophaga_tridactyla/)
  quote: “Their weight is born on the knuckles and wrist to protect the claws.” (13 words).
  funFactQuote: “Using the long, sharp claws on their forelimbs, they open insect colonies and tree trunks.” (15 words).

## B13-0378 — How does a three-banded armadillo completely enclose its body in its own armor?

Answer: Roll into a complete ball. Fun fact: Its shell can close around the body.

- animals-nature-s0056: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/la-plata-three-banded-armadillo)
  quote: “allowing the head, legs and tail to tuck inside when the animal rolls into a ball.” (16 words).
- animals-nature-s0052: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Tolypeutes_matacus/)
  quote: “It can completely close its shell around its entire body.” (10 words).

## B13-0379 — What conspicuous protective covering does a three-banded armadillo have?

Answer: Body armor. Fun fact: The armored covering extends to the head and tail.

- animals-nature-s0056: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/la-plata-three-banded-armadillo)
  quote: “The tail, top of head and the outer surfaces of the limbs are also usually armored.” (16 words).
- animals-nature-s0052: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Tolypeutes_matacus/)
  quote: “They are dark brown and heavely armored with a thick, leathery shell” (12 words).
  funFactQuote: “This armor covers the tail, head, feet, and back of the animal.” (12 words).

## B13-0380 — Southern three-banded armadillos are native to which continent?

Answer: South America. Fun fact: Their native range includes several South American countries.

- animals-nature-s0056: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/la-plata-three-banded-armadillo)
  quote: “Native to South America” (4 words).

## B13-0381 — What prey do southern three-banded armadillos mainly eat?

Answer: Ants and termites. Fun fact: Strong limbs and claws help obtain their food.

- animals-nature-s0056: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/la-plata-three-banded-armadillo)
  quote: “In the wild they feed primarily on ants and termites, which they obtain using their powerful forelegs and claws.” (19 words).
- animals-nature-s0052: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Tolypeutes_matacus/)
  quote: “This species of armadillo eats mainly ants and termites.” (9 words).
  funFactQuote: “They use their strong legs and large claws to dig through insect colonies” (13 words).

## B13-0382 — Ring-tailed lemurs are native to which island?

Answer: Madagascar. Fun fact: Their native range includes the island’s southwest.

- animals-nature-s0043: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/ring-tailed-lemur)
  quote: “Ring-tailed lemurs live in southwestern Madagascar” (6 words).
- animals-nature-s0044: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Lemur_catta/)
  quote: “Ring-tailed lemurs are found only in southern and southwestern Madagascar.” (10 words).

## B13-0383 — Which colors alternate in a ring-tailed lemur’s tail bands?

Answer: Black and white. Fun fact: The bands run along its long tail.

- animals-nature-s0043: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/ring-tailed-lemur)
  quote: “alternating black and white bands that adorn their tails.” (9 words).
- animals-nature-s0044: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Lemur_catta/)
  quote: “The tail has thick, well defined, black and white rings from stem to tip.” (14 words).

## B13-0384 — What do ring-tailed lemurs waft at opponents during a “stink battle”?

Answer: Scent from their tails. Fun fact: They rub gland secretions onto the tail before displaying it.

- animals-nature-s0043: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/ring-tailed-lemur)
  quote: “Secretions from the wrist glands can also be rubbed on the tail and flicked at an opponent.” (17 words).
- animals-nature-s0044: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Lemur_catta/)
  quote: “secretions from scent glands are rubbed onto the tail, then wafted at opposing animals.” (14 words).

## B13-0385 — Which sex typically dominates a ring-tailed lemur troop’s hierarchy?

Answer: Females. Fun fact: Females typically remain in their birth group.

- animals-nature-s0043: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/ring-tailed-lemur)
  quote: “Females are dominant within groups, meaning females have preferential access to food” (12 words).
  funFactQuote: “Females spend their whole lives in their birth group.” (9 words).
- animals-nature-s0044: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Lemur_catta/)
  quote: “All females are dominant over all males.” (7 words).
  funFactQuote: “Females stay with the same troop they were born into” (10 words).

## B13-0386 — What is the characteristic coat color of a golden lion tamarin?

Answer: Reddish-gold. Fun fact: A mane frames its face and head.

- animals-nature-s0045: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/golden-lion-tamarin)
  quote: “Golden lion tamarins are small, social primates with reddish-gold coats and long, backswept manes.” (14 words).
- animals-nature-s0046: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Leontopithecus_rosalia/)

## B13-0387 — Golden lion tamarins are native to coastal rainforest in which country?

Answer: Brazil. Fun fact: Their habitat is associated with the Atlantic coast.

- animals-nature-s0045: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/golden-lion-tamarin)

## B13-0388 — How many infants does a female golden lion tamarin usually deliver at a time?

Answer: Twins. Fun fact: A typical litter consists of two young.

- animals-nature-s0045: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/golden-lion-tamarin)
  quote: “the female usually gives birth to twins.” (7 words).
- animals-nature-s0046: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Leontopithecus_rosalia/)
  quote: “Females give birth to twins after a gestation of 130 to 135 days.” (13 words).

## B13-0389 — Golden lion tamarins belong to which broad animal group?

Answer: Primates. Fun fact: They are small monkeys with a mane around the face.

- animals-nature-s0045: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/golden-lion-tamarin)
  quote: “Golden lion tamarins are small, social primates” (7 words).
